⏺ The async poll fights GDB's batch-mode event model — interrupt requested but
  is_running() never flipped, so my code tried to resume an already-running
  target. Let me back out the polling complexity and rely on the outer-timeout
  bump alone (the kernel already writes per-suite progress to serial, and
  gdb-serial.log is uploaded as a CI artifact so the diagnostic info is
  preserved either way).

  Searched for 2 patterns, read 1 file, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Slices 8 + 9 together are a big body of work — full sigaction table + kill() +
   delivery semantics + default actions + interactive picker, plus
  reentrancy-safe schedule() + per-task ticks + runtime quantum + two ktests.
  Realistically multiple PRs, not one.

  Recommended first wedge: signal subsystem core, no preemption work yet — add
  sigaction/handler table to task_t, implement kill() (SYS=37) + signal()
  (SYS=48) syscalls, wire delivery on the syscall-return path, and convert the
  existing g_sigint/keyboard_sigint_consume Ctrl+C path to deliver a real SIGINT
   to the focused task. That's a self-contained PR that unblocks everything else
   (the picker app and the preemption ktests both want real signal delivery).

  Want me to go that route, or pick a different starting wedge (e.g. preemption
  hardening first, since it's smaller and independent)?

✻ Crunched for 46s

❯ go for it                                                                     

⏺ Starting the signal core. Plan: add signal.h/signal.c with sig_send +

      654 +    KTEST_ASSERT(sig_default_terminates(SIGCONT) == 0);       
      655 +

          +;

      590          break;
      591      }
      592  
      593 +    /* -------------------------------------------------------
          +-----------                                                   
      594 +     * SYS_KILL(37): deliver a signal to a target pid.        
      595 +     * EBX = pid, ECX = signo.  Returns 0 on success, -1 on er
          +ror                                                           
      596 +     * (no such pid, or invalid signo).                       
      597 +     *                                                        
      598 +     * No permission model yet -- any task can signal any othe
          +r.  When                                                      
      599 +     * a user/kernel split lands, this will become the natural
          + choke                                                        
      600 +     * point for credential checks.                           
      601 +     * -------------------------------------------------------
          +----------- */                                                
      602 +    case SYS_KILL: {                                          
      603 +        int pid   = (int)regs->ebx;                           
      604 +        int signo = (int)regs->ecx;                           
      605 +        if (signo < 1 || signo > SIG_MAX) {                   
      606 +            regs->eax = (uint32_t)-1;                         
      607 +            break;                                            
      608 +        }                                                     
      609 +        regs->eax = (sig_send_pid(pid, signo) == 0)           
      610 +                    ? 0u : (uint32_t)-1;                      
      611 +        break;                                                
      612 +    }                                                         
      613 +                                                              
      614 +    /* -------------------------------------------------------
          +-----------                                                   
      615 +     * SYS_SIGNAL(48): install a handler for signo on the call
          +ing task.                                                     
      616 +     * EBX = signo, ECX = handler (SIG_DFL = 0, SIG_IGN = 1, o
          +r a                                                           
      617 +     * user-space function pointer).                          
      618 +     *                                                        
      619 +     * Returns the previous handler value, or (uint32_t)-1 on 
          +error                                                         
      620 +     * (out-of-range signo, or signo == SIGKILL/SIGSTOP).     
      621 +     *                                                        
      622 +     * User-defined handlers are stored but not yet invoked (n
          +o ring-3                                                      
      623 +     * trampoline + sigreturn machinery yet).  SIG_DFL and SIG
          +_IGN take                                                     
      624 +     * effect immediately because the in-kernel delivery path 
          +checks                                                        
      625 +     * those sentinels by pointer identity.                   
      626 +     * -------------------------------------------------------
          +----------- */                                                
      627 +    case SYS_SIGNAL: {                                        
      628 +        int signo = (int)regs->ebx;                           
      629 +        sig_handler_t new_h = (sig_handler_t)(uintptr_t)regs->
          +ecx;                                                          
      630 +        task_t *t = task_current();                           
      631 +        if (!t || signo < 1 || signo > SIG_MAX ||             
      632 +            signo == SIGKILL || signo == SIGSTOP) {           
      633 +            regs->eax = (uint32_t)-1;                         
      634 +            break;                                            
      635 +        }                                                     
      636 +        /* We don't have a getter on signal.c yet; reading the
          + previous                                                     
      637 +         * disposition requires a probe via sig_set_handler re
          +turning                                                       
      638 +         * the prior value.  For this first cut we report SIG_
          +DFL when                                                      
      639 +         * the previous value is unknown; userspace that needs
          + the real                                                     
      640 +         * previous disposition can use sigaction once that la
          +nds. */                                                       
      641 +        sig_handler_t prev = SIG_DFL;                         
      642 +        if (sig_set_handler(t, signo, new_h) != 0) {          
      643 +            regs->eax = (uint32_t)-1;                         
      644 +            break;                                            
      645 +        }                                                     
      646 +        regs->eax = (uint32_t)(uintptr_t)prev;                
      647 +        break;                                                
      648 +    }                                                         
      649 +                                                              
      650      default:
      651          /* Unknown syscall - return -ENOSYS. */
      652          regs->eax = (uint32_t)-38;   /* -ENOSYS */

⏺ Three commits landed on feat/signals-preemption:

  ┌─────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

  └─────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Hit a design fork before I keep going. The next phase is Ctrl+C → SIGINT 
  migration (drop g_sigint / keyboard_sigint_consume). Two paths:

  1. Replace g_sigint outright — keyboard ISR delivers SIGINT to the
  focused-TTY's task; the shell installs SIG_IGN for SIGINT and switches its
  readline-abort path to read \x03 from the keyboard ring instead of polling a
  global. Cleanest end state, but touches keyboard.c + shell readline + every
  keyboard_sigint_consume caller, and the behaviour change risks ui-test
  regressions.
  2. Parallel path — keyboard ISR sets g_sigint and sends SIGINT to focused
  task. Shell keeps polling g_sigint; child ELFs get terminated by the kernel
  via sig_deliver instead of by the shell's force-kill loop. Smaller diff, but
  g_sigint lives on as duplicate state.

  I'd recommend (1) for the right end state, but stop here for direction since
  it's a behaviour change worth confirming. Which do you want?

✻ Brewed for 5m 6s

※ recap: Working slices 8+9 (signals + preemption) on feat/signals-preemption; signal core, SYS_KILL, and SYS_SIGNAL are committed and iso-test is green. Next: pick path 1 (replace g_sigint) or path 2 (parallel) for the Ctrl+C → SIGINT migration. (disable recaps in /config)

      34  static int     tasking_active  = 0;
      35  static int     next_pid        = 2;   /* idle task gets PID 1; 
          created tasks start at 2 */
      36  
      37 +/* schedule() re-entrancy guard.  Single-CPU only; an MP port w
         +ould need                                                      
      38 + * this per-CPU and protected by a different primitive.  Set to
         + 1 across                                                      
      39 + * schedule()'s critical section; checked at entry to bail on n
         +ested                                                          
      40 + * calls (either an IRQ that managed to land during the pre-cli
         + window                                                        
      41 + * or some future kernel code that calls task_yield() from insi
         +de one of                                                      
      42 + * the helpers schedule() invokes).                            
      43 + *                                                             
      44 + * Without this, the scheduler relied entirely on cli/sti disci
         +pline:                                                         
      45 + * the timer IRQ runs with IF=0 from isr_common_stub, so re-ent
         +ry via                                                         
      46 + * IRQ was already prevented in practice -- but the guarantee w
         +as                                                             
      47 + * implicit and one stray sti would have re-opened the hole.  T
         +he flag                                                        
      48 + * makes the invariant explicit so future code can't accidental
         +ly undo                                                        
      49 + * it. */                                                      
      50 +static volatile int in_schedule = 0;                           
      51 +                                                               
      52 +/* Save EFLAGS and disable interrupts; return the prior EFLAGS 
         +so the                                                         
      53 + * caller can restore exactly the IF state it had on entry. */ 
      54 +static inline uint32_t irq_save_disable(void)                  
      55 +{                                                              
      56 +    uint32_t f;                                                
      57 +    asm volatile("pushfl; popl %0; cli" : "=r"(f) :: "memory");
      58 +    return f;                                                  
      59 +}                                                              
      60 +                                                               
      61 +static inline void irq_restore(uint32_t f)                     
      62 +{                                                              
      63 +    asm volatile("pushl %0; popfl" :: "r"(f) : "memory", "cc");
      64 +}                                                              
      65 +                                                               
      66  /* ------------------------------------------------------------
          -------------
      67   * init_task_stack
      68   *

⏺ Found it: my re-entrancy flag is set at entry but a fresh task's stack frame
  (from init_task_stack) doesn't run schedule()'s epilogue — so in_schedule
  stays 1 forever after the first new-task switch. Fix: clear the flag before
  task_switch, not after.

⏺ Pushed. PR #154 now has the two slice 9 commits added (c4f9299 phase 1 +
  3d9388a docs).

  What's left of slice 9 (from issue #145):

  Phase: 2
  What: Per-task tick accounting — add uint32_t kticks to task_t, bump in
    timer_callback on task_current() before yielding, expose in /proc/tasks
  Size: small (~30 LoC + format change)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Phase: 3
  What: Runtime-tunable SCHED_QUANTUM — convert from #define in timer.c to a
    variable, add a knob (shell builtin sched_quantum <n> or
  /proc/sched_quantum,
     or both)
  Size: small (~50 LoC)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Phase: 4
  What: Busy-loop ktest (one task pinned at 100% CPU via for(;;), verify
    another's kticks still advances) + concurrent-yield stress (N tasks
  hammering
     task_yield, verify no crash / no infinite loop / in_schedule flag stays
    sane)
  Size: medium (~80 LoC; needs phase 2's tick counter to assert "still
    scheduled")

  Phases 2 and 4 are tightly coupled (4 needs the counter from 2). Phase 3 is
  independent and easy to bolt on alongside either.

  Want me to keep going? My suggestion: bundle 2+4 next (counter + the test that
   validates the whole hardening story), then 3 last so the picker is the
  smallest possible add. Or stop here and let PR #154 land first?

           ----------------
      753 + * Suite: preempt

          +ob: a victim                                                  
      756 + * task busy-loops with no task_yield, the test task yields no
          +rmally, and

          + missing

          +tays flat

      764 +static volatile int preempt_run = 0;

      766 +static void test_preempt_busy_loop(void)                      
      767 +{                                                             
      768 +    /* No task_yield: only the PIT IRQ can preempt us.  Cleare
          +d from                                                        
      769 +     * the parent so we exit cleanly even if the SIGKILL teard
          +own                                                           
      770 +     * below somehow doesn't fire. */                         
      771 +    while (preempt_run)                                       
      772 +        ;                                                     
      773 +    task_exit();                                              
      774 +}                                                             
      775 +                                                              
      776 +static void test_preempt(void)                                
      777 +{                                                             
      778 +    ktest_begin("preempt",                                    
      779 +                "timer-driven preemption: a busy-loop task doe
          +s not "                                                       
      780 +                "starve the rest of the system, kticks counter
          + advances");                                                  
      781 +                                                              
      782 +    task_t *cur = task_current();                             
      783 +    KTEST_ASSERT(cur != NULL);                                
      784 +                                                              
      785 +    preempt_run = 1;                                          
      786 +    task_t *v = task_create("preempt_victim", test_preempt_bus
          +y_loop);                                                      
      787 +    KTEST_ASSERT(v != NULL);                                  
      788 +    if (!v) { preempt_run = 0; ktest_summary(); return; }     
      789 +                                                              
      790 +    uint32_t my_before = cur->kticks;

✻ Sautéed for 3m 40s

❯ 8 phase 4?                                                                    

⏺ Phase 4 is the ring-3 signal trampoline + sigreturn syscall so user-installed
  signal(SIGINT, my_handler) actually invokes my_handler in ring 3. Currently
  those handlers are stored but the kernel just leaves the pending bit set (the
  comment in sig_deliver is honest about it).

  It's a meaningfully bigger lift than slices 8.1–8.3 were:

  1. Detect "returning to ring 3" — hook the path in isr_asm.S / syscall.c
  that's about to iret back to user mode, before the iret runs.
  2. Build a sigframe on the user stack — push the interrupted register state
  (CS/EIP/EFLAGS/ESP/SS + general regs), signo, return address pointing at the
  trampoline.
  3. Trampoline in userspace — small piece of code (~6 instructions) that the
  handler returns into, which then invokes SYS_SIGRETURN. Conventionally placed
  at a fixed address or in a vDSO-like region; we'd hand-roll it in crt0.S for
  now since we don't have a libc.
  4. SYS_SIGRETURN — restore the saved frame and resume the interrupted
  instruction.
  5. Edge cases — nested signals, mask handling during handler execution, signal
   sent while in kernel (deliver on next ring-3 return), interaction with the
  existing sig_deliver-in-schedule() path (default actions should still fire for
   the no-handler case).

  Realistically 4-6 hours of careful work + debugging, plus a ring-3 ktest that
  installs a handler and verifies it actually runs. Easy to break ring-3
  lifecycle in the process.

  Recommendation: don't attempt in this session given token budget. The signal
  core is genuinely useful as-is — kill, default-terminate, Ctrl+C all work for
  ring-3 tasks; only user-defined handlers are non-functional. Land PR #154
  first, file phase 4 as its own issue, come back to it on a fresh branch with a
   full budget.

  Want me to file the GitHub issue describing phase 4 and what it needs?

✻ Crunched for 27s

❯ Try it, we've pushed the PR in a working state, so nothing lost if we run out

⏺ Designing the sigframe + trampoline + sigreturn. I'll be efficient — single
  commit, minimal testing till the end.

⏺ Update(src/kernel/include/kernel/signal.h)  ⎿  Added 47 lines
       99   * ktest. */
      100  int sig_default_terminates(int signo);
      101  
      102 +/* -----------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------                                              
      103 + * Ring-3 signal trampoline (slice 8 phase 4)                 
      104 + *                                                            
      105 + * When a user-defined handler is installed for a pending unbl
          +ocked                                                         
      106 + * signal, signal_check_user (called at the end of isr_handler
          + /                                                            
      107 + * irq_handler, i.e. just before iret to ring 3) builds a sigf
          +rame on                                                       
      108 + * the calling task's user stack, redirects regs->eip to the h
          +andler,                                                       
      109 + * and lets iret run.  The handler returns into the trampoline
          + embedded                                                     
      110 + * at the bottom of the sigframe, which invokes SYS_SIGRETURN.
      111 + * SYS_SIGRETURN reads the sigframe back from the user stack a
          +nd                                                            
      112 + * restores the interrupted state so the next iret resumes the
          + original                                                     
      113 + * ring-3 instruction.                                        
      114 + * -----------------------------------------------------------
          +---------------- */                                           
      115 +                                                              
      116 +#define SIGFRAME_MAGIC 0x53494746u   /* "SIGF" */             
      117 +                                                              
      118 +/* On-user-stack frame layout.  Kept compact and 4-byte aligne
          +d.  Do not                                                    
      119 + * reorder ret_addr / signo: the handler is invoked via iret w
          +ith esp                                                       
      120 + * pointing at ret_addr and reads signo at [esp+4] (cdecl). */
      121 +typedef struct sigframe {                                     
      122 +    uint32_t ret_addr;        /* points at trampoline[0] below
          +          */                                                  
      123 +    uint32_t signo;           /* handler's first arg          
          +          */                                                  
      124 +    uint32_t saved_eip;                                       
      125 +    uint32_t saved_cs;                                        
      126 +    uint32_t saved_eflags;                                    
      127 +    uint32_t saved_useresp;                                   
      128 +    uint32_t saved_ss;                                        
      129 +    uint32_t saved_eax;                                       
      130 +    uint32_t saved_ebx;                                       
      131 +    uint32_t saved_ecx;                                       
      132 +    uint32_t saved_edx;                                       
      133 +    uint32_t saved_esi;                                       
      134 +    uint32_t saved_edi;                                       
      135 +    uint32_t saved_ebp;                                       
      136 +    uint32_t saved_ds;                                        
      137 +    uint32_t magic;           /* SIGFRAME_MAGIC               
          +          */                                                  
      138 +    uint8_t  trampoline[8];   /* movl $SYS_SIGRETURN,%eax; int
          + $0x80    */                                                  
      139 +} sigframe_t;                                                 
      140 +                                                              
      141 +struct registers;                                             
      142 +                                                              
      143 +/* Called from isr.c at the end of every interrupt dispatch.  
          +No-op                                                         
      144 + * unless the interrupted frame is ring 3, the calling task ha
          +s an                                                          
      145 + * unmasked pending signal, and that signal has a non-default 
      146 + * non-ignore handler installed. */                           
      147 +void signal_check_user(struct registers *regs);

⏺ Update(src/kernel/arch/i386/proc/signal.c)
  ⎿  Added 104 lines
      207      }
      208  }
      209  
      210 +/* -----------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------                                              
      211 + * Ring-3 trampoline delivery (slice 8 phase 4)               
      212 + *                                                            
      213 + * Called from isr.c at the end of every interrupt dispatch.  
          +If the                                                        
      214 + * interrupted frame is ring 3 AND the calling task has a deli
          +verable                                                       
      215 + * (pending & unmasked) signal with a user-installed handler, 
          +we build                                                      
      216 + * a sigframe on the user stack and redirect regs->eip to the 
          +handler.                                                      
      217 + * The handler returns into the trampoline at the bottom of th
          +e                                                             
      218 + * sigframe, which executes SYS_SIGRETURN to restore state.   
      219 + *                                                            
      220 + * Default-action / SIG_IGN cases are handled at schedule()'s 
      221 + * sig_deliver instead -- they don't need a user-stack trampol
          +ine,                                                          
      222 + * just kernel-side state mutation.  Those run before we ever 
          +return                                                        
      223 + * to user mode, so a default-terminate task never reaches thi
          +s                                                             
      224 + * function (it's TASK_DEAD by then).                         
      225 + * -----------------------------------------------------------
          +---------------- */                                           
      226 +                                                              
      227 +/* Trampoline bytes: movl $SYS_SIGRETURN, %eax; int $0x80; nop
          +.                                                             
      228 + * Constant so the compiler embeds it in .rodata; we memcpy at
          + delivery. */                                                 
      229 +static const uint8_t s_sigreturn_trampoline[8] = {            
      230 +    0xb8,                                                     
      231 +    (uint8_t)(SYS_SIGRETURN      ),                           
      232 +    (uint8_t)(SYS_SIGRETURN >>  8),                           
      233 +    (uint8_t)(SYS_SIGRETURN >> 16),                           
      234 +    (uint8_t)(SYS_SIGRETURN >> 24),                           
      235 +    0xcd, 0x80,                                               
      236 +    0x90,                                                     
      237 +};                                                            
      238 +                                                              
      239 +void signal_check_user(registers_t *regs)                     
      240 +{                                                             
      241 +    /* Only consider tasks about to iret to ring 3.  The CS lo
          +w two                                                         
      242 +     * bits encode the requested privilege level; user code se
          +gments                                                        
      243 +     * are RPL=3 (0x1B == GDT index 3 | RPL 3). */            
      244 +    if ((regs->cs & 0x3u) != 0x3u)                            
      245 +        return;                                               
      246 +                                                              
      247 +    struct task *t = task_current();                          
      248 +    if (!t)                                                   
      249 +        return;                                               
      250 +                                                              
      251 +    uint32_t deliverable = t->sig_pending & ~t->sig_mask;     
      252 +    if (!deliverable)                                         
      253 +        return;                                               
      254 +                                                              
      255 +    for (int sig = 1; sig <= SIG_MAX; sig++) {                
      256 +        uint32_t bit = SIG_BIT(sig);                          
      257 +        if (!(deliverable & bit))                             
      258 +            continue;                                         
      259 +        sig_handler_t h = sig_get_handler(t, sig);            
      260 +        /* SIG_DFL and SIG_IGN are sig_deliver()'s job; SIGKIL
          +L has no                                                      
      261 +         * handler override.  Anything else is a user pointer.
          + */                                                           
      262 +        if (h == SIG_DFL || h == SIG_IGN)                     
      263 +            continue;                                         
      264 +        if (sig == SIGKILL)                                   
      265 +            continue;                                         
      266 +                                                              
      267 +        /* Place sigframe just below the current user esp.  Th
          +e page is                                                     
      268 +         * mapped (it's the ring-3 stack page) so direct write
          +s via the                                                     
      269 +         * same CR3 are safe.  Sigframe is ~70 bytes, well und
          +er one                                                        
      270 +         * page, so we don't cross the stack-page boundary in 
          +practice. */                                                  
      271 +        uint32_t base = regs->useresp - sizeof(sigframe_t);   
      272 +        sigframe_t *uf = (sigframe_t *)(uintptr_t)base;       
      273 +                                                              
      274 +        uf->ret_addr     = base + (uint32_t)offsetof(sigframe_
          +t, trampoline);                                               
      275 +        uf->signo        = (uint32_t)sig;                     
      276 +        uf->saved_eip    = regs->eip;                         
      277 +        uf->saved_cs     = regs->cs;                          
      278 +        uf->saved_eflags = regs->eflags;                      
      279 +        uf->saved_useresp= regs->useresp;                     
      280 +        uf->saved_ss     = regs->ss;                          
      281 +        uf->saved_eax    = regs->eax;                         
      282 +        uf->saved_ebx    = regs->ebx;                         
      283 +        uf->saved_ecx    = regs->ecx;                         
      284 +        uf->saved_edx    = regs->edx;                         
      285 +        uf->saved_esi    = regs->esi;                         
      286 +        uf->saved_edi    = regs->edi;                         
      287 +        uf->saved_ebp    = regs->ebp;                         
      288 +        uf->saved_ds     = regs->ds;                          
      289 +        uf->magic        = SIGFRAME_MAGIC;                    
      290 +        memcpy(uf->trampoline, s_sigreturn_trampoline,        
      291 +               sizeof(s_sigreturn_trampoline));               
      292 +                                                              
      293 +        /* Redirect iret to the handler with esp at the sigfra
          +me head.                                                      
      294 +         * Handler is invoked cdecl with [esp]=ret_addr, [esp+
          +4]=signo. */                                                  
      295 +        regs->useresp = base;                                 
      296 +        regs->eip     = (uint32_t)(uintptr_t)h;               
      297 +                                                              
      298 +        /* Clear pending so we don't re-deliver before the han
          +dler                                                          
      299 +         * even gets a chance to run. */                      
      300 +        t->sig_pending &= ~bit;                               
      301 +                                                              
      302 +        Serial_WriteString("[signal] deliver sig=");          
      303 +        Serial_WriteDec((uint32_t)sig);                       
      304 +        Serial_WriteString(" pid=");                          
      305 +        Serial_WriteDec((uint32_t)t->pid);                    
      306 +        Serial_WriteString(" handler=0x");                    
      307 +        Serial_WriteHex((uint32_t)(uintptr_t)h);              
      308 +        Serial_WriteString("\n");                             
      309 +                                                              
      310 +        return;   /* one signal per return-to-user pass */    
      311 +    }                                                         
      312 +}                                                             
      313 +                                                              
      314  int sig_check_and_clear(int signo)
      315  {
      316      if (signo < 1 || signo > SIG_MAX)

      642          break;
      643      }
      644  
      645 +    /* -------------------------------------------------------
          +-----------                                                   
      646 +     * SYS_SIGRETURN(119): restore interrupted state from the 
          +sigframe                                                      
      647 +     * on the user stack.  Invoked indirectly by the trampolin
          +e embedded                                                    
      648 +     * in the sigframe -- userspace should never call this dir
          +ectly.                                                        
      649 +     *                                                        
      650 +     * On entry, regs->useresp points at the slot immediately 
          +after                                                         
      651 +     * ret_addr (i.e. signo's old slot), because the handler's
          + `ret`                                                        
      652 +     * already popped ret_addr.  The sigframe starts at useres
          +p - 4.                                                        
      653 +     *                                                        
      654 +     * On a corrupted / out-of-range sigframe we terminate the
          + task                                                         
      655 +     * rather than try to limp on with whatever state we recov
          +er --                                                         
      656 +     * sigreturn from a missing or tampered frame is a recover
          +y dead                                                        
      657 +     * end.                                                   
      658 +     * -------------------------------------------------------
          +----------- */                                                
      659 +    case SYS_SIGRETURN: {                                     
      660 +        task_t *t = task_current();                           
      661 +        uint32_t base = regs->useresp - 4u;                   
      662 +        /* Range check: sigframe must lie inside the user stac
          +k page                                                        
      663 +         * range we manage.  USER_STACK_TOP is the top of the 
          +one                                                           
      664 +         * mapped 4 KiB stack page (stack grows down from ther
          +e). */                                                        
      665 +        if (base < (USER_STACK_TOP - 4096u) ||                
      666 +            base + sizeof(sigframe_t) > USER_STACK_TOP) {     
      667 +            Serial_WriteString("[signal] sigreturn: out-of-ran
          +ge frame; "                                                   
      668 +                               "killing pid=");               
      669 +            Serial_WriteDec(t ? (uint32_t)t->pid : 0u);       
      670 +            Serial_WriteString("\n");                         
      671 +            if (t) t->state = TASK_DEAD;                      
      672 +            /* Fall through to task_yield -- we can't iret bac
          +k to user. */                                                 
      673 +            task_yield();                                     
      674 +            break;                                            
      675 +        }                                                     
      676 +        sigframe_t sf;                                        
      677 +        memcpy(&sf, (const void *)(uintptr_t)base, sizeof(sf))
          +;                                                             
      678 +        if (sf.magic != SIGFRAME_MAGIC) {                     
      679 +            Serial_WriteString("[signal] sigreturn: bad magic 
          +0x");                                                         
      680 +            Serial_WriteHex(sf.magic);                        
      681 +            Serial_WriteString("; killing pid=");             
      682 +            Serial_WriteDec(t ? (uint32_t)t->pid : 0u);       
      683 +            Serial_WriteString("\n");                         
      684 +            if (t) t->state = TASK_DEAD;                      
      685 +            task_yield();                                     
      686 +            break;                                            
      687 +        }                                                     
      688 +        regs->eip      = sf.saved_eip;                        
      689 +        regs->cs       = sf.saved_cs;                         
      690 +        regs->eflags   = sf.saved_eflags;                     
      691 +        regs->useresp  = sf.saved_useresp;                    
      692 +        regs->ss       = sf.saved_ss;                         
      693 +        regs->eax      = sf.saved_eax;                        
      694 +        regs->ebx      = sf.saved_ebx;                        
      695 +        regs->ecx      = sf.saved_ecx;                        
      696 +        regs->edx      = sf.saved_edx;                        
      697 +        regs->esi      = sf.saved_esi;                        
      698 +        regs->edi      = sf.saved_edi;                        
      699 +        regs->ebp      = sf.saved_ebp;                        
      700 +        regs->ds       = sf.saved_ds;                         
      701 +        break;                                                
      702 +    }                                                         
      703 +                                                              
      704      default:
      705          /* Unknown syscall - return -ENOSYS. */
      706          regs->eax = (uint32_t)-38;   /* -ENOSYS */
      707          break;
      708      }
      709 +                                                              
      710 +    /* Slice 8 phase 4: deliver any user-handler signal that's
          + pending                                                      
      711 +     * (and unmasked) before iret returns to ring 3.  No-op wh
          +en the                                                        
      712 +     * frame is ring 0 or no handler is installed. */         
      713 +    signal_check_user(regs);

❯ What's flaky? 

⏺ Pushed. PR #154 now has slice 8 phase 4.

  About the flake — earlier run had glob-proc / tab-path / cd-root / per-tty-cwd
   / calc-brackets all failing with the same pattern: scenario typed its command
   (e.g. cat /proc/*<Enter>), the kernel logged task pid=N status=0 -> 
  task_exit() for the child ELF, but the child's actual stdout was missing from
  the serial slice. The very same scenarios passed in isolation, passed with
  explicit args, and passed on the re-run with no args. Three retries → green
  every time after the one bad run.

  What I think happened: the failing scenarios all rely on g_serial_verbose
  being on (set by start_qemu typing verbose on<Enter> after boot), so shell
  output is mirrored to COM1. The passing scenarios (exec-hello,
  user-sigusr1-handler, makbox-pwd) write to serial directly via sys_write to fd
   2 or sys_write_serial from the ELF itself — they don't depend on the
  tty→serial mirror. My phase-4 hook runs signal_check_user at the end of every
  ISR/IRQ dispatch. If a Ctrl+C between tests happened to land in a narrow
  window during the verbose on<Enter> setup keystrokes — interrupting
  cmd_verbose mid-execution — g_serial_verbose might have been left at the wrong
   value for that whole run.

  That's a hypothesis I can't prove without instrumentation, and since it's
  irreproducible after the fact, I'm going to leave it alone unless it recurs.
  If you want, yes, please grab a ui-test-gui screenshot the next time it
  misbehaves — visible window during the failing scenarios will make the actual
  VT0 state obvious (whether the shell is at a prompt, whether the prior
  command's output is on screen, etc.) and rule in/out the "shell got stuck" vs
  "output went to serial that wasn't captured" theories.

      551 +    { 20, "SIGTSTP"  },                                       
      552 +};                                                            
      553 +                                                              
      554 +#define PICKER_W 20                                           
      555 +                                                              
      556 +#define CLR_PICK_HDR  VGA_CLR(VGA_BLACK,  VGA_LGREEN)         
      557 +#define CLR_PICK_ROW  VGA_CLR(VGA_LGREY,  VGA_BLACK)          
      558 +#define CLR_PICK_SEL  VGA_CLR(VGA_BLACK,  VGA_LCYAN)          
      559 +#define CLR_PICK_HINT VGA_CLR(VGA_LCYAN,  VGA_BLACK)          
      560 +                                                              
      561 +static void draw_picker(int sel, int target_pid)              
      562 +{                                                             
      563 +    char tmp[16];                                             
      564 +    int total = (int)(sizeof(SIGNALS)/sizeof(SIGNALS[0]));    
      565 +                                                              
      566 +    /* Header row across the picker width. */                 
      567 +    put_pad(0, 0, PICKER_W, CLR_PICK_HDR);                    
      568 +    put_str(1, 0, "Send signal:", CLR_PICK_HDR);              
      569 +    /* Show target PID compactly on the same header line if th
          +ere's room. */                                                
      570 +    {                                                         
      571 +        uitoa((unsigned int)target_pid, tmp);                 
      572 +        unsigned int n = slen(tmp);                           
      573 +        int col = PICKER_W - (int)n - 1;                      
      574 +        if (col > 13) put_str(col, 0, tmp, CLR_PICK_HDR);     
      575 +    }                                                         
      576 +                                                              
      577 +    /* Body rows: scroll so sel is always visible.  ROW_FOOTER
          + and                                                          
      578 +     * one above it are reserved for the hint line.  We don't 
          +draw on                                                       
      579 +     * the kernel's status row (s_rows is the pane height; ker
          +nel                                                           
      580 +     * status bar lives at row s_rows). */                    
      581 +    int body_top    = 1;                                      
      582 +    int body_bottom = s_rows - 2;       /* leave one row for h
          +int */                                                        
      583 +    int body_h      = body_bottom - body_top + 1;             
      584 +    int top = sel - body_h / 2;                               
      585 +    if (top < 0) top = 0;                                     
      586 +    if (top > total - body_h) top = total - body_h;           
      587 +    if (top < 0) top = 0;                                     
      588 +                                                              
      589 +    for (int row = body_top; row <= body_bottom; row++) {     
      590 +        int idx = top + (row - body_top);                     
      591 +        unsigned char clr = (idx == sel) ? CLR_PICK_SEL : CLR_
          +PICK_ROW;                                                     
      592 +        put_pad(0, row, PICKER_W, clr);                       
      593 +        if (idx < 0 || idx >= total) continue;                
      594 +        uitoa((unsigned int)SIGNALS[idx].signo, tmp);         
      595 +        /* Right-align signo in cols 1..3. */                 
      596 +        int slot = 3 - (int)slen(tmp);                        
      597 +        if (slot < 1) slot = 1;                               
      598 +        put_str(slot, row, tmp, clr);                         
      599 +        put_str(5, row, SIGNALS[idx].name, clr);              
      600 +    }                                                         
      601 +                                                              
      602 +    /* Hint row at the bottom of the picker. */               
      603 +    put_pad(0, s_rows - 1, PICKER_W, CLR_PICK_HINT);          
      604 +    put_str(1, s_rows - 1, " up/dn  ent send ", CLR_PICK_HINT)
          +;                                                             
      605 +                                                              
      606 +    flush();                                                  
      607 +}                                                             
      608 +                                                              
      609 +static int signal_picker(int target_pid)                      
      610 +{                                                             
      611 +    int sel = 13;   /* index of SIGTERM in the table -- safe d
          +efault */                                                     
      612 +    int total = (int)(sizeof(SIGNALS)/sizeof(SIGNALS[0]));    
      613 +                                                              
      614 +    invalidate_prev();    /* picker overlays the underlying vi
          +ew */                                                         
      615 +    draw_picker(sel, target_pid);                             
      616 +                                                              
      617 +    for (;;) {                                                
      618 +        int c = wait_key();                                   
      619 +        if (c == 0x1B || c == 'q' || c == 'Q') return -1;     
          +/* Esc / q */                                                 
      620 +        if (c == '\n' || c == '\r') return SIGNALS[sel].signo;
      621 +        if (c == KEY_ARROW_UP   && sel > 0)            { sel--
          +; draw_picker(sel, target_pid); }                             
      622 +        else if (c == KEY_ARROW_DOWN && sel < total-1) { sel++
          +; draw_picker(sel, target_pid); }                             
      623 +    }                                                         
      624 +}                                                             
      625 +                                                              
      626  #define REFRESH_TICKS 50u
      627  
      628  int main(int argc, char **argv, char **envp)

❯ [Image #1] why is CPU at 100% and mem at 0M?                                  
  ⎿  [Image #1]

  Searched for 1 pattern, read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Two bugs:

  1. 0M/0M: /proc/meminfo doesn't expose MemTotal — only
  MemFree/PageSize/FreeFrames/HeapTotal/HeapUsed/HeapFree. My maktop parser
  reads MemTotal and gets 0.
  2. 100% CPU: at any timer tick some task is current_task (even idle), so
  total-kticks-delta always equals real-ticks-delta = 100% utilisation. Need to
  exclude idle from the "busy" count.

  Fixing both. First, add MemTotal to procfs.

  Searched for 4 patterns, read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

❯ also what's the ktest no-op stuff for? If a task is dead, why is it still     
  showing?                                                                      

⏺ That's the boot-time ktest task (ktest_bg) — runs the kernel test suite in the
   background while the loading screen is up, sets ktest_bg_done=1 when
  finished, then exits cleanly. It shows up because /proc/tasks renders DEAD 
  tasks too — they linger until task_create reclaims the slot (Makar has no
  wait()/zombie reaper).

  Filtering DEAD from /proc/tasks so maktop (and cat /proc/tasks) only show live
   tasks.

  ⎿  Interrupted · What should Claude do instead?

❯ Task switching is a bit weird. I switched to VT4, switched back and the       
  colours were messed and not responding to keyboard input, so I had to switch  
  out and shutdown.                                                             
    Things are bugs you should be picking up. Also, when entering a wrong       
  command, the shell just clears instead of carrying on.                        

⏺ Those are good catches. The "typo clears screen" is my fault — I made
  shell_clear_screen() unconditional after every exec, so when shell falls back
  to makbox <typo> and makbox exits with an error, screen clears. The
  VT-switching issue is pre-existing (kernel doesn't track "active focus per VT"
   — exec'd children get focus once, but vtty_switch only restores to the shell,
   not the running child).

  Quick fix for the typo issue: only clear screen if the child actually touched
  the framebuffer (via sys_putch_at/sys_tty_clear). Tracked via a per-task flag.

❯ What is the flake? 

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The flake: timing race. Send-script types 14 keys + waits 1.2s. The typo runs
  an exec (makbox), prints error, dies. Then pwd types and runs another exec.
  Under TCG (no KVM), two exec round-trips plus the dispatching shell takes
  longer than 1.2s — dd snapshots the serial slice while pwd is still mid-type.
  The slice ends with root@makar /~> p instead of completing pwd<Enter>.

  Fix: give this scenario more wait time.

❯ I also want to think of a better way to do this. Sendkey is really good, but  
  you seem to be hampered by it - how do other OSes do this kinda UI-oriented   
  automated testing?                                                            

⏺ Good question — sendkey-plus-fixed-sleep is the most fragile end of the
  spectrum. What real OS projects do, roughly in increasing sophistication:

  1. Expect-style "wait for marker" (most common)

  Buildroot, Yocto's oeqa, Genode's run-scripts, and Linux-from-scratch test
  rigs all use pexpect (Python) or Tcl Expect. The pattern is:

  send "ls /etc\n"
  expect "# "          ← block until the prompt regex appears, NO fixed sleep
  send "next thing\n"

  The shell prints #  (or whatever) after ls finishes; the test syncs on that.
  Fast paths exit in 50 ms, slow paths still pass because the wait is bounded by
   the prompt, not by a guess. This is what fixes our flake — we'd sync on
  root@makar /~> between keystrokes instead of guessing 1.2s.

  2. In-kernel test sentinels

  Linux's KUnit, FreeBSD's ATF, and Genode all instrument the kernel to print
  structured markers (e.g., KTEST_BG: PASS, which Makar already does for the
  boot ktest harness). The test driver greps the marker, never the human-visible
   UI. Most reliable, but requires the kernel to cooperate.

  3. GDB-stub-driven testing

  QEMU has a gdb stub; the test sets breakpoints at known kernel symbols and
  steps deterministically. Exactly what tests/gdb_boot_test.py already does for
  the boot-checkpoints / hardware-state / bg-ktest groups. Zero timing reliance.

  4. Image-diff / golden screenshots

  GUI projects (KDE, GNOME end-to-end suites, Microsoft's accessibility tests)
  take PPM screendumps and diff against reference images. Makar's ui_runner
  already takes a .ppm per scenario — we just don't compare. Useful for "did vix
   render correctly" type checks where serial doesn't capture pixel state.

  5. Virtio / paravirtual test channels

  virtme-ng, virt-test, and modern Linux test infra mount a 9p filesystem from
  host into guest, then the test inside the guest just writes a file the host
  watches. No keyboard simulation at all.

  ---
  For Makar specifically, biggest leverage with smallest effort:

  ┌──────┬────────────────────────────────┬────────┬───────────────────────┐
  │ Tier │             Change             │ Effort │    Pays off where     │
  ├──────┼────────────────────────────────┼────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │      │ Add wait_for_serial <regex>    │        │ Eliminates 90% of our │
  │ A    │ [timeout] to ui_runner.sh; use │ ~30    │  flakes (the          │
  │      │  between sendkey batches       │ LoC    │ typo-doesnt-clear     │
  │      │                                │        │ case)                 │
  ├──────┼────────────────────────────────┼────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │      │ Emit [shell:ready vt=N] to     │ ~5 LoC │ Gives tier-A a stable │
  │ B    │ serial in shell_readline on    │        │  prompt marker, no    │
  │      │ entry                          │ kernel │ false matches         │
  ├──────┼────────────────────────────────┼────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │      │ Wire ppm screendump diff       │ ~50    │ Fullscreen apps (vix, │
  │ C    │ (compare against               │ LoC    │  maktop) get          │

      230  CURRENT_DUMP=""

      233 +# wait_for_serial <pattern> <start_bytes> [timeout_s=5]       
      234 +#   Polls $SERIAL_LOG for the first match of <pattern> in the 
          +slice                                                         
      235 +#   starting at byte offset <start_bytes>.  Returns 0 when fou
          +nd, 1 on                                                      
      236 +#   timeout.  Used by `it_until` (and by tests directly) to re
          +place                                                         
      237 +#   fixed sleeps with "sync on a marker" -- the expect/pexpect
          + idiom.                                                       
      238 +wait_for_serial() {                                           
      239 +    local pattern=$1                                          
      240 +    local start_bytes=$2                                      
      241 +    local timeout=${3:-5}                                     
      242 +    local deadline=$(($(date +%s) + timeout))                 
      243 +    while [ $(date +%s) -lt $deadline ]; do                   
      244 +        if [ -f "$SERIAL_LOG" ] && \                          
      245 +           dd if="$SERIAL_LOG" bs=1 skip="$start_bytes" 2>/dev
          +/null \                                                       
      246 +              | grep -qE -- "$pattern"; then                  
      247 +            return 0                                          
      248 +        fi                                                    
      249 +        sleep 0.1                                             
      250 +    done                                                      
      251 +    return 1                                                  
      252 +}                                                             
      253 +                                                              
      254 +# it_until <label> <sendkey-script> <sync-pattern> [timeout_s=
          +5]                                                            
      255 +#   Like `it`, but instead of sleeping a fixed duration after 
          +the keys                                                      
      256 +#   are sent, polls the serial log until <sync-pattern> appear
          +s (or                                                         
      257 +#   the timeout elapses).  The shell's `shell_readline` emits 
      258 +#   `[shell:ready vt=N]` on entry, so `'\[shell:ready vt=0\]'`
          + is the                                                       
      259 +#   natural sync marker for "the shell is back at the prompt".
      260 +it_until() {                                                  
      261 +    CURRENT_NAME=$1                                           
      262 +    local script=$2                                           
      263 +    local pattern=$3                                          
      264 +    local timeout=${4:-5}                                     
      265 +                                                              
      266 +    reset_shell                                               
      267 +                                                              
      268 +    local start_bytes=0                                       
      269 +    if [ -f "$SERIAL_LOG" ]; then                             
      270 +        start_bytes=$(wc -c < "$SERIAL_LOG")                  
      271 +    fi                                                        
      272 +                                                              
      273 +    send_script "$script"                                     
      274 +                                                              
      275 +    if ! wait_for_serial "$pattern" "$start_bytes" "$timeout";
          + then                                                         
      276 +        echo "  - wait_for_serial timed out waiting for: $patt
          +ern"                                                          
      277 +    fi                                                        
      278 +                                                              
      279 +    CURRENT_SEGMENT=$LOGDIR/$CURRENT_NAME.serial              
      280 +    CURRENT_DUMP=$LOGDIR/$CURRENT_NAME.ppm                    
      281 +    CURRENT_FAILED=0                                          
      282 +    rm -f "$CURRENT_SEGMENT" "$CURRENT_DUMP"                  
      283 +    echo "screendump $CURRENT_DUMP" | nc -U "$MONITOR_SOCK" >/
          +dev/null                                                      
      284 +    sleep 0.2                                                 
      285 +                                                              
      286 +    dd if="$SERIAL_LOG" bs=1 skip="$start_bytes" 2>/dev/null >
          + "$CURRENT_SEGMENT"                                           
      287 +}                                                             
      288 +                                                              
      289  # it <label> <sendkey-script> [extra-wait-secs]
      290  #   Resets the shell, drives the keystrokes, waits long enough
            for the
      291  #   command(s) to complete, snapshots the per-test serial slic
           e into

  Read 1 file, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
⏺ Found the real bug: keys arrive at the typo's exec'd child task (focused),
  then the child dies, the shell calls keyboard_release_task which drops the 
  in-flight bytes, then refocuses to itself — so p of pwd gets eaten. Solution:
  type batch-by-batch with wait_for_serial between.

       17 +### Added — signals + preemption hardening (PR #154, slices 8 
          ++ 9)                                                          
       18 +                                                              
       19 +- **Per-task signal subsystem** (`src/kernel/include/kernel/si
          +gnal.h`,                                                      
       20 +  `src/kernel/arch/i386/proc/signal.c`).  Linux i386 signal nu
          +mbers,                                                        
       21 +  `sig_handler_t` typedef, `SIG_DFL`/`SIG_IGN` sentinels.  Per
          +-task                                                         
       22 +  handler table held in a static array indexed by task-pool sl
          +ot.                                                           
       23 +  Default-action classifier follows Linux defaults; SIGKILL by
          +passes                                                        
       24 +  mask + handler; SIGKILL/SIGSTOP can't have their disposition
       25 +  overridden.  Delivery hooks at `schedule()` (for default-ter
          +minate                                                        
       26 +  and SIG_IGN) and at the return-to-ring-3 path in `isr_handle
          +r` /                                                          
       27 +  `irq_handler` / `syscall_dispatch` (for user-installed handl
          +ers via                                                       
       28 +  a sigframe trampoline on the user stack).                   
       29 +- **Syscalls**:                                               
       30 +    - `SYS_KILL(37)` — `kill(pid, signo)`.  Linux i386 number.
       31 +    - `SYS_SIGNAL(48)` — `signal(signo, handler)`.  Returns th
          +e                                                             
       32 +      previous handler.                                       
       33 +    - `SYS_SIGRETURN(119)` — sigframe restore; invoked indirec
          +tly by                                                        
       34 +      the trampoline embedded at the bottom of every sigframe.
       35 +    - `SYS_FCNTL(55)` — `F_GETFL` / `F_SETFL` with `O_NONBLOCK
          +` on                                                          
       36 +      stdin so non-blocking reads return `-EAGAIN` instead of 
       37 +      blocking via `shell_readline`.                          
       38 +- **`task_t.unkillable`** flag: idle (pid 1) and shell tasks s
          +et this,                                                      
       39 +  and `sig_deliver` drops SIGKILL / default-terminate signals 
          +on                                                            
       40 +  unkillable targets instead of marking them DEAD.  Without it
          +,                                                             
       41 +  maktop's F9-picker / a stray `kill` from the shell could lea
          +ve a                                                          
       42 +  VT permanently dead.                                        
       43 +- **Per-task `kticks`** PIT-tick accounting, incremented by   
       44 +  `timer_callback` and rendered in `/proc/tasks` (slice 9 phas
          +e 2).                                                         
       45 +- **Runtime-tunable scheduling quantum** — `g_sched_quantum` (
          +default                                                       
       46 +  4 PIT ticks = 40 ms slice) writable via the new `sched_quant
          +um`                                                           
       47 +  shell builtin in `[1..100]` (slice 9 phase 3).              
       48 +- **`maktop.elf`** — htop-style task viewer.  Resolution-agnos
          +tic via                                                       
       49 +  `sys_term_cols`/`sys_term_rows`.  Linux-style /proc/meminfo 
          ++                                                             
       50 +  /proc/tasks parsing, horizontal CPU% + memory meter bars (gr
          +een →                                                         
       51 +  yellow → red), tmux-style preserved status bar at the bottom
          + row.                                                         
       52 +  Auto-refreshes every 1 s; ↑↓ navigate, F9 opens a left-side 
          +signal                                                        
       53 +  picker (↑↓ pick a name, Enter sends), `s` is a numeric-entry
       54 +  alternate, `r` forces refresh, F10 / `q` quits.  Stays      
       55 +  POSIX-aligned: stdin set to `O_NONBLOCK` via `fcntl`, `read`
          + returns                                                      
       56 +  `-EAGAIN` when the keyboard ring is empty.                  
       57 +- **`sigtest.elf`** — ring-3 verifier for `sys_signal` + sigfr
          +ame +                                                         
       58 +  sigreturn.  Installs SIGUSR1 handler, self-sends, asserts th
          +e                                                             
       59 +  handler ran.                                                
       60 +- **`/proc/meminfo` Linux-style fields** — `MemTotal`, `MemFre
          +e`,                                                           
       61 +  `MemAvailable`, `MemUsed`, plus existing heap stats.  `pmm_i
          +nit`                                                          
       62 +  caches the bootloader-managed frame count as the source of  
       63 +  `MemTotal`.                                                 
       64 +- **`task_t.fb_touched`** — set by `SYS_PUTCH_AT` / `SYS_TTY_C
          +LEAR`                                                         
       65 +  on the calling task.  `shell_exec_elf` checks it after the c
          +hild                                                          
       66 +  dies and only calls `shell_clear_screen()` for fullscreen ap
          +ps,                                                           
       67 +  so line-mode children (cat, hello, makbox-fallback on typos)
       68 +  don't have their output wiped from the screen.              
       69 +- **`in_schedule` re-entrancy guard** in `schedule()`, paired 
          +with                                                          
       70 +  `irq_save_disable` / `irq_restore` around the critical secti
          +on.                                                           
       71 +  Cleared *before* `task_switch` so fresh tasks (whose first  
       72 +  execution bypasses the schedule epilogue) don't leave the fl
          +ag                                                            
       73 +  set forever (slice 9 phase 1).                              
       74 +- **ktest:**

       76 +      sig_send/sig_deliver, SIGKILL override of SIG_IGN, sig_s
          +end_pid,

⏺ Update(docs/testing.md)
  ⎿  Added 48 lines
      118  
      119  ---
      120  
      121 +## Black-box UI tests (`./run.sh ui-test`)                    
      122 +                                                              
      123 +Drives the running kernel through QEMU's HMP `sendkey` and ass
          +erts on                                                       
      124 +the COM1 serial slice + PPM screen dump.  Scenarios live in   
      125 +`tests/ui_test.sh` as `test_<name>` shell functions; the share
          +d runner                                                      
      126 +in `tests/ui_runner.sh` boots one QEMU per invocation and rota
          +tes                                                           
      127 +through scenarios with `reset_shell` in between.              
      128 +                                                              
      129 +### Synchronisation: marker-based, not sleep-based            
      130 +                                                              
      131 +The shell emits `[shell:ready vt=N]` to serial on every       
      132 +`shell_readline` entry (gated by `g_serial_verbose`, which    
      133 +`start_qemu` flips on as the first action after boot).  Test  
      134 +scenarios use this marker as a sync point instead of fixed `sl
          +eep N`:                                                       
      135 +                                                              
      136 +| Primitive | What it does |                                  
      137 +|---|---|                                                     
      138 +| `it <name> <script> [wait_secs]` | Fixed-sleep style.  Send 
          +keys, sleep, snapshot serial slice. |                         
      139 +| `it_until <name> <script> <regex> [timeout]` | **Sync-on-mar
          +ker style.**  Send keys, poll the serial log until the regex a
          +ppears, then snapshot.  Default sync regex is `'\[shell:ready 
          +vt=0\]'`. |                                                   
      140 +| `wait_for_serial <regex> <start_bytes> [timeout]` | Low-leve
          +l primitive.  Polls `$SERIAL_LOG` from `<start_bytes>` until `
          +<regex>` matches.  Used by `it_until` and directly by multi-st
          +age scenarios that need to sync between key batches (the `typo
          +-doesnt-clear` scenario is the canonical multi-stage example: 
          +type the typo, wait for shell-ready, then type the next comman
          +d, otherwise the in-flight bytes race the dying exec'd child a
          +nd get dropped when `keyboard_release_task` reaps its slot). |
      141 +                                                              
      142 +This is the expect/pexpect pattern, scaled down.  Why it matte
          +rs: a                                                         
      143 +fixed `sleep 1.2` is a guess that's right under KVM and wrong 
          +under                                                         
      144 +TCG-on-a-shared-runner.  A marker sync is right in both -- it 
          +returns                                                       
      145 +the instant the kernel says it's ready and bounds via the time
          +out.                                                          
      146 +                                                              
      147 +### Scenarios currently covered                               
      148 +                                                              
      149 +| Scenario | Asserts |                                        
      150 +|---|---|                                                     
      151 +| `glob-proc` | `cat /proc/*` glob-expands across the syntheti
          +c FS |                                                        
      152 +| `tab-complete-path` | `cat<TAB> /proc/c<TAB><Enter>` resolve
          +s to `cat /proc/cpuinfo` |                                    
      153 +| `exec-hello` | `exec /cdrom/apps/hello.elf tester` reaches `
          +sys_exit(0)` and prints the expected greeting |               
      154 +| `cd-root-listing` | `cd /<TAB><TAB>` lists mounts; subsequen
          +t `pwd` confirms cwd |                                        
      155 +| `per-tty-cwd` | Per-task cwd isolation across `Alt+F1`/`Alt+
          +F3` switches |                                                
      156 +| `calc-brackets` | `calc.elf` evaluates parenthesised arithme
          +tic |                                                         
      157 +| `ctrlc-kills-child` | Ctrl+C delivers SIGINT to a running EL
          +F, shell recovers |                                           
      158 +| `no-dead-in-proctasks` | `cat /proc/tasks` never lists DEAD 
          +slots |                                                       
      159 +| `typo-doesnt-clear` | A wrong command falls back to makbox, 
          +prints an error, and **does not** wipe the screen (proves the 
          +`task_t.fb_touched` gate) |                                   
      160 +| `user-sigusr1-handler` | `sigtest.elf` installs a SIGUSR1 ha
          +ndler, self-sends, the handler runs in ring 3 (proves the sigf
          +rame + trampoline + `SYS_SIGRETURN` path) |                   
      161 +| `makbox-pwd` | `pwd` resolves to the `makbox` applet end-to-
          +end |                                                         
      162 +                                                              
      163 +`./run.sh ui-test` runs all of them; `./run.sh ui-test <name>`
          + runs                                                         
      164 +one; `./run.sh ui-test-gui` runs with a visible QEMU window fo
          +r                                                             
      165 +debugging.                                                    
      166 +                                                              
      167 +---                                                           
      168 +                                                              
      169  ## CI
      170  
      171  `.github/workflows/build-test.yml` runs a **build-once, fan-ou
           t** topology on every push to `main` and every PR. Docs-only c
           hanges are skipped via path filter.

⏺ Real bug — vtty_switch routes focus to the slot owner (shell0), not the
  foreground exec'd child (maktop). Plus maktop's diff render keeps a stale
  prev[] across the switch, so it only repaints changed cells over whatever was
  last on FB (the destination VT's content). Fixing both, then adding a ui-test
  scenario.
